removes audit-trail context. The deleted table documented, per state, the cleanup owner (e.g.,
`StopGuard::drop` vs. panic-safety for `SchedGuard`), test-isolation pattern (per-test `manage` vs.
remaining serial lock), the "intentionally process-level" carve-outs (`NEXT_STOP_ID`,
`SESSION_ORIGINS`) with measured grep evidence (notably the "value never read" finding that gated an
independent refactor), and the "do not containerize" set with reasons. Future maintainers touching
`AppState` or adding a new containerized table have no on-disk signpost for these decisions — they
must `git log -p app_state.rs` to recover it. While the new module-level doc preserves a high-level
pointer, the concrete cleanup ownership, RAII semantics, and audit findings now exist only in git
log. This is a regression in auditability rather than a refactor. Consider keeping at minimum the
"intentionally not containerized" four-item list as a table in-file, or moving it to a top-level
design-notes doc rather than relying on git log.

─── src-tauri/src/bot/config/commands.rs:355-357 ───
[bug · critical] CRITICAL — `bot_set_active_model` now holds `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK` via `_g =
io::lock_config_write()` and then calls `bot_get_config(app)` at the tail, but `bot_get_config`
(commands.rs:42, :52) invokes `io::migrate_legacy_key(&app)` / `io::migrate_search_keys(&app)`, and
both of those call `lock_config_write()` internally (io.rs:543, :578). `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK` is a
`std::sync::Mutex` (not reentrant); `lock_config_write()` blindly calls `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK.lock()`
without consulting the thread-local `HOLDING_CONFIG_WRITE` flag, so the second acquire on the same
thread blocks forever → deadlock on every 🧠 下拉切模型动作. Fix: either (a) drop `_g` before calling
`bot_get_config`, or (b) reconstruct the view inline without re-running `bot_get_config` (the
model_id switch only touches `models_by_provider` / `active_model_id` / `api_provider` / legacy
`base_url,model`), or (c) make `lock_config_write` honor the existing thread-local guard and return
a no-op guard when `HOLDING_CONFIG_WRITE` is true.

  io::write_bot_config_file_locked(&crate::db::data_dir(&app), cfg)?;
+     // _g 必须在此处 drop：bot_get_config 内部会再调 io::migrate_legacy_key /
+     // io::migrate_search_keys，两者各自 lock_config_write()，std Mutex 不可重入
+     // 会立即死锁。释放后再读视图足够——磁盘就是我们刚写完的状态。
+     drop(_g);
      bot_get_config(app)
  }

─── src-tauri/src/api_server.rs:159-170 ───
[bug · high] History mutex poisoning is silently skipped (log+continue) while id persistence failure
is strictly fail-closed (return early). This creates a consistency gap: when `history.lock()`
returns Err, the event id is already persisted to disk AND broadcast to live clients (lines 172-184
still execute), but the event is NOT added to history. Any client that was not live during this
broadcast (or reconnects with `since < id`) will never see this event via `replay()`. This breaks
the replay consistency contract that `broadcast_persist_failure_fails_closed` (line 327)
establishes: events that don't reach history should also not reach clients. Either fall back to
`e.into_inner()` to match the clients-lock handling at line 141, or `return` early here (consuming
the id like the persistence fail-closed path does — though that introduces a different inconsistency
with the persisted id).

Concretely, the broadcast flow currently is:
- id allocated (line 144)
- id persisted (line 151-157) → fail-closed on Err
- history push (line 159-170) → silently skipped on Err ← inconsistent
- client push (line 172-184) → runs unconditionally

The third step's poison handling should align with one of the two existing strategies (fail-closed
like persistence, or recover like the clients lock) rather than inventing a third "best-effort" path
that leaves the event sent to clients but absent from history.

- +        match self.history.lock() {
- +            Ok(mut h) => {
- +                h.push_back((id, msg.clone()));
- +                while h.len() > EVENT_HISTORY {
- +                    h.pop_front();
- +                }
- +            }
- +            Err(e) => {
- +                // 保持 if let Ok 语义：中毒不 into_inner 抢救，只留痕跳过入史
- +                eprintln!("[mutex_poisoned] api_server::broadcast history: {e:?}");
- +            }
- +        }
+         // 与 id 持久化一致的 fail-closed：history 中毒时事件也不投递，
+         // 否则会出现「id 已落盘+已推在线客户端，但 history 里没有」的不可恢复 gap。
+         if let Err(e) = self.history.lock() {
+             eprintln!("[mutex_poisoned] api_server::broadcast history: {e:?}");
+             // 与落盘 fail-closed 同思路：归还 id + 不入史 + 不推送，
+             // 保「要么持久化要么不推进」的契约；下次 broadcast 重取同 id 重试
+             self.next_id.fetch_sub(1, Ordering::SeqCst);
+             return;
+         }
+         let mut h = self.history.lock().unwrap();
+         h.push_back((id, msg.clone()));
+         while h.len() > EVENT_HISTORY {
+             h.pop_front();
+         }


─── src-tauri/src/api_server.rs:166-169 ───
[maintainability · low] The comment claims "保持 if let Ok 语义" (preserve `if let Ok` semantics), but
the change does NOT strictly preserve the original behavior — the original `if let Ok` silently
dropped on Err, while the new code logs and continues. The comment is misleading and the rationale
("中毒不 into_inner 抢救") doesn't address the inconsistency with the clients-lock recovery at line 141,
which DOES use `into_inner()` after logging. Either explain why history deliberately chooses NOT to

─── src-tauri/src/bot/config/io.rs:164-174 ───
[other · high] Cross-lock data-loss race: `write_memory_tuning_at` uses its own module-local
`TUNING_WRITE_LOCK`, which is independent of the existing `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK` that serializes
`add_allowed_dir` / `update_config_file` / `migrate_legacy_key` / `migrate_search_keys` /
`write_bot_config_file_locked`. Two concurrent writers to the same file (e.g. a memoryTuning tweak
while a key migration runs) both load the file, both succeed independently, and the second
`atomic_write` silently overwrites the first writer's unrelated field changes. The header comment
defers this as "既有已登记 follow-up" but the race is real and observable today. Recommended fix: wrap
this function's RMW inside the same `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK` that the rest of the file's writers use
(e.g. `lock_config_write()`), or fold `TUNING_WRITE_LOCK` into `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK` and have all RMW
callers go through it. Declaring the static inside the function body is also unusual — hoist it next
to `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK` for consistency with the rest of the module.

  pub fn write_memory_tuning_at(
      path: &Path,
      tuning: Option<crate::memory::MemoryTuning>,
  ) -> Result<(), String> {
-     /// bot-config.json 的 tuning RMW 全程锁（本文件内多写者互斥；
-     /// 跨文件写者统一锁 = 既有已登记 follow-up，与 set_apply_policy 同口径）
-     static TUNING_WRITE_LOCK: std::sync::Mutex<()> = std::sync::Mutex::new(());
-     let _g = TUNING_WRITE_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| {
-         eprintln!("[mutex_poisoned] bot::config::io::TUNING_WRITE_LOCK: {e:?}");
-         e.into_inner()
-     });
+     let _g = lock_config_write();

─── src-tauri/src/bot/config/io.rs:661-701 ───
[maintainability · low] Test placement is unusual: the new
`write_memory_tuning_roundtrip_preserves_and_removes` is declared *before* `use super::*;` inside
`mod tests`. It compiles only because every symbol is fully qualified
(`crate::memory::MemoryTuning`, etc.). This works, but it inverts the conventional `use ...; #[test]
fn ...` ordering and silently breaks any future maintainer who adds `use super::*;`-dependent
asserts above it (they'd just see a confusing "use must come first" compiler error later). Move the
`use super::*;` line above the new `#[test] fn` block for convention; the test currently doesn't use
anything from `super` via the glob so the order is purely cosmetic, but the convention matters for
future edits.

+     use super::*;
+ 
      #[test]
      fn write_memory_tuning_roundtrip_preserves_and_removes() {
          let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!(
              "mem-tuning-{}",
              chrono::Utc::now().timestamp_nanos_opt().unwrap_or(0)
          ));
          std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();
          let p = dir.join("bot-config.json");
          std::fs::write(
              &p,
              r#"{"baseUrl":"https://x/v1","evolution":{"applyPolicy":"confirm"}}"#,
          )
          .unwrap();
  
          // Some：写入（超界值被钳制），兄弟/顶层字段保留
          let t = crate::memory::MemoryTuning {
              top_n: 99,
              ..Default::default()
          };
          write_memory_tuning_at(&p, Some(t)).unwrap();
          let v: serde_json::Value =
              serde_json::from_str(&std::fs::read_to_string(&p).unwrap()).unwrap();
          assert_eq!(v["memoryTuning"]["topN"], 10, "超界钳到上限");
          assert_eq!(v["baseUrl"], "https://x/v1", "顶层字段保留");
          assert_eq!(v["evolution"]["applyPolicy"], "confirm", "兄弟块保留");
  
          // None：整块移除，其余保留
          write_memory_tuning_at(&p, None).unwrap();
          let v: serde_json::Value =
              serde_json::from_str(&std::fs::read_to_string(&p).unwrap()).unwrap();
          assert!(v.get("memoryTuning").is_none());
          assert_eq!(v["baseUrl"], "https://x/v1");
  
          // 坏文件拒绝写
          std::fs::write(&p, "{ broken").unwrap();
          assert!(write_memory_tuning_at(&p, None).is_err());
          assert_eq!(std::fs::read_to_string(&p).unwrap(), "{ broken");
          let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir);
      }
-

─── src-tauri/src/bot/config/io.rs:168-174 ───
[maintainability · low] The file now has two lock-declaration styles side-by-side: `pub(crate)
static CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK: Mutex<()> = Mutex::new(());` declared at module scope next to
`lock_config_write()`, and `static TUNING_WRITE_LOCK: std::sync::Mutex<()> =
std::sync::Mutex::new(());` declared inside the function body. The function-body style works in Rust
(statics are hoisted to module scope), but it's stylistically inconsistent and obscures the fact
that this is a second module-level lock — making it harder for a future reader to spot the
cross-lock race. Hoist it to module scope next to `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK` (and, per the related
high-severity finding, fold it into `CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK`).

-     /// bot-config.json 的 tuning RMW 全程锁（本文件内多写者互斥；
-     /// 跨文件写者统一锁 = 既有已登记 follow-up，与 set_apply_policy 同口径）
-     static TUNING_WRITE_LOCK: std::sync::Mutex<()> = std::sync::Mutex::new(());
-     let _g = TUNING_WRITE_LOCK.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| {
-         eprintln!("[mutex_poisoned] bot::config::io::TUNING_WRITE_LOCK: {e:?}");
-         e.into_inner()
-     });
+     let _g = lock_config_write();

─── src-tauri/src/bot/config/schema.rs:306-309 ───
[performance · medium] 这条 `eprintln!` 加在 `load_config` 热路径上会形成持续噪音。`migrate_bot_config_schema` 是
`load_config` 的前置钩子，且本文件自身的注释明确写了 "load_config 是热路径，避免每次读盘解析"；任何时候只要有 RMW 写路径（`update_config_file` /
`add_allowed_dir` / `mcp_server_save` / `bot_set_config` 等）持锁运行，并发的 `load_config` 都会走 `WouldBlock`
分支——而 MCP dispatch / 聊天循环 / 设置面板都会反复读 `load_config`，一次写动作期间可能触发几十次 `load_config`，全部都会刷一行
stderr，遮蔽真正的 mutex_poisoned 行（紧邻的另一处 `eprintln!`）。要么把日志条件化（类似 `[mutex_poisoned]` 只在非预期路径打印），要么改用
`tracing::debug!` / `log::debug!`，要么删除该行（保持迁移让路无音正是设计意图）。

设计复核：本 diff 给 `WouldBlock` 加日志，却对同函数紧邻的 `Poisoned` 分支只调
`e.into_inner()`——两边都是「不让步」语义，但行为却一个喧闹一个沉默，统一策略更可读（例如：让路 = 静默，poison = 必然告警）。

          Err(std::sync::TryLockError::WouldBlock) => {
-             eprintln!("[bot] schema 迁移让路：CONFIG_WRITE_LOCK 持锁中，持锁写路径会补跑");
+             // 让路不丢迁移：持锁写路径进段已先调 migrate_bot_config_schema_locked。
+             // 静默返回 —— load_config 是热路径，写盘持锁期间可能反复命中此分支，
+             // 任何每调用日志都会刷爆 stderr 并遮蔽邻近的 mutex_poisoned 告警。
              return Ok(());
          }


─── src-tauri/src/bot/config/io.rs:190-195 ───
[other · low] Cross-file observation: the newly re-exported `write_memory_tuning_at` (io.rs)
contains a no-op block in the `None` branch — `if let Some(evo) = obj.get_mut("memoryTuning") { let
_ = evo; }` immediately precedes `obj.remove("memoryTuning")` and does nothing (acquires a mut ref
and discards it). The block should be deleted; the `obj.remove("memoryTuning")` call below already
handles the absent-key case. Keeping it dead risks confusing future readers about whether the
removed key needs special handling.



─── src-tauri/src/api_handlers/body.rs:94-99 ───
[bug · high] 新增的「非法 UTF-8 → BodyRead::Malformed」会把原本只代表 header 异常(多 Content-Length / parse 失败 /
Transfer-Encoding 出现)的语义污染成「body 字节非法」。两个调用点 (handlers.rs:222, handlers.rs:402) 不区分原因，统一返回
`"malformed Content-Length header"` —— 客户端发了一个 Content-Length 完好的 body,只因 body 含非法 UTF-8
字节就被告知"header 格式不对",日志/排错都会被误导。需要新增独立变体(如 `BodyRead::InvalidUtf8`)或放宽 `Malformed` 的调用方报错文案(改为
`"malformed request"` 之类不含具体原因的中性词),并在调用处分别发对应 400 信息。

-     // 损坏内容入库比当场拒绝更糟 → 归入 Malformed（调用方回 400）

─── src-tauri/src/bot/config/types.rs:582-588 ───
[security · high] The switch from `read_llm_key(api_provider, active_model_id, models_by_provider)`
to `crate::bot::config::keyring::read_api_key()` correctly closes the cross-vendor credential leak
the previous code had: when an override entry's vendor key is missing the fallback no longer reaches
for the *currently active* model's vendor key and sends it to the entry's own `base_url`. The intent
matches the audit recommendation and the new comment is accurate.

However, the fallback swallows **any** `Err` from `read_vendor_key`, including a real keyring fault
for the entry's *own* vendor (locked keychain, permission denied, dbus session gone). In that case
the request silently routes to the global main key, which can be an unrelated vendor's credential —
i.e. the same class of leak the diff is otherwise fixing, just shifted one layer. The audit's
suggested fix (classify `NoEntry` / 未配置 as fallback, propagate other `CommandError::KeyringError`)
was not applied here. Recommend narrowing the fallback arm to only `NoEntry` / 未配置 and propagating
real keyring faults so the user gets a clear error instead of a silent cross-vendor request.

This is independent of the cross-vendor leak fix and worth flagging because the diff is otherwise
exactly the right change.

─── src-tauri/src/bot/mcp/secrets.rs:300-300 ───
[documentation · medium] The function doc at line 300 still claims "无「先 0644 后 chmod」窗口" (no 'first
0644 then chmod' window), but the new implementation does exactly that: it first writes through
`OpenOptions::open` (which only applies `mode(0o600)` on file *creation*) and then chmods via
`set_permissions` afterwards. With a leftover tmp file (e.g. 0644 from a prior crash), secrets are
now briefly world-readable between `write_all` and `set_permissions`. Update the doc to reflect the
actual contract — e.g. "tmp 文件 0600 写入：新建即 0600；复用残留文件时落 rename 前显式收紧一次" — so future readers aren't
misled about the TOCTOU window.



─── src-tauri/src/bot/mcp/secrets.rs:313-322 ───
[security · medium] The new `set_permissions` is added *after* `f.write_all(bytes)`, but for a
leftover tmp file the mode is still loose during the write. Between `open` (reusing a 0644 tmp) and
`set_permissions`, the tmp file contains plaintext secrets with world-readable permissions. Consider
tightening first (or calling `create_new(true)` and unlinking on failure) so the secrets never touch
a 0644 inode, matching the original "无 chmod 窗口" intent. Note also the inner `#[cfg(unix)]` is
redundant — its enclosing block is already `#[cfg(unix)]`.

[maintainability · low] Capture-and-truncate threshold mixes byte-length and char-count units:
`line.len() > 4000` is a UTF-8 byte check while `chars().take(4000)` is a Unicode-scalar-value cap.
For a multi-byte-heavy line whose byte length exceeds 4000 but whose char count is below 4000 (e.g.,
~1.7k CJK chars ≈ 5 KB), `take(4000)` collects everything unchanged and `t.push('…')` then *grows*
the String by 3 bytes. The allocation is still bounded (worst case ≈ 16 KB at 4-byte chars × 4000),
but the doc-comment 「4k 字符截断」 is misleading, and the trigger threshold reads inconsistently.
Consider matching units (e.g., switch the trigger to `line.chars().count() > 4000`, or — preferred —
find a valid UTF-8 boundary via `char_indices` and truncate on bytes directly with a
`str::from_utf8`-safe index).

+                             // 分配，超出按字节级安全边界截断到 ≤4kB，只影响尾部

─── src-tauri/src/bot/tools.rs:1499-1501 ───
[bug · medium] Silent template fallback hides user-misnamed input: `word_template_resolve` returns
`Option<String>` (`document.rs:1335`) — when the model passes an explicit `template` name that does
not exist on disk, the resolver falls all the way back to `None` ("无模板可用，生成走内置空白默认"). The call site
has no way to detect this: the success string still reads "已生成 Word 文档" with no hint. Same hazard
for `doc_make_ppt` (`document.rs:1247-1250`, `pptx_template_resolve` → `builtin_pptx_master`). A
user who deliberately picked "年度报告-v2.docx" gets a generic blank deck with zero diagnostic.
Suggestion: when `template` was explicitly provided but resolved to `None`, pass an explicit
`template: Some(unresolved_name)` sentinel into the Python script and let it log/audit

─── src-tauri/src/bot/tools.rs:1521-1523 ───
[security · low] Unsafe integer arithmetic on user-controlled file size: `m.len() / 1024` produces
the file size in KiB without a guard against pathological sizes. While `document.rs` already
verified the file is non-empty, it did not cap the upper bound; in theory a malicious or runaway
Python script could produce a multi-GB "已生成" file and the new format string `"，{} KB"` would print
`，4294967296 KB` for a 4 GiB artifact. Numerically fine (`u64 / u64`) but the UX lies about the
unit: 4 GiB ≠ "4294967296 KB" is functionally correct but reads strangely; more importantly,
displaying size in KiB for large files is misleading. Consider formatting by power-of-2 unit
(KB/MB/GB) or pairing it with `m.len()` in bytes when over a threshold. Also note: this new code
path is the *only* place the user sees an artifact size, so any rounding/scaling bug here becomes
the documented UX.

              let size_note = std::fs::metadata(&out)
-                 .map(|m| format!("，{} KB", m.len() / 1024))
+                 .map(|m| {
+                     let b = m.len();
+                     if b < 1024 * 1024 {
+                         format!("，{} KB", b / 1024)
+                     } else {
+                         format!("，{:.1} MB", b as f64 / 1024.0 / 1024.0)
+                     }
+                 })
                  .unwrap_or_default();

claim. `execute_mcp_direct("", ...)` falls through `parsed.is_object() == true` (the empty-string
branch yields `json!({})`) and reaches `shared().call_tool(...)`, which in turn calls
`ensure_connected → spawn_service`. With `cfg = McpServerConfig::default()` this leaves a
`Slot::Down` at key `""` in the global `MANAGER.slots` (process-global `OnceLock`). Two follow-on
concerns: (a) the assertion is purely negative (`!out.text.contains("必须是 JSON 对象")`) and depends on
whatever text `spawn_service` happens to produce for an empty-command config — a future change to
that error message could silently flip the test to no longer exercise the empty branch meaningfully;
(b) the leftover slot is benign for `find_mounted` (which uses `mcp_mounted_tools()` and only emits
Connected slots) but pollutes the global state with no `cleanup`. Either drop the empty-string
sanity check (or move it to a separate, isolated test), or refactor to assert a stronger signal —
e.g. before calling `execute_mcp_direct` on the empty case assert that the global slots map has no
`""` key, and after call_tool-for-default-cfg-fails verify that key was inserted, so the test
documents and pins the side effect instead of hiding it.

[security · medium] [medium] Regex-mode `applied` is written via `atomic_write` with no size guard;
only `applied.is_empty() && !content.is_empty()` is checked. A regex that matches many positions
(e.g. `\b`, `(\w)`) combined with a non-trivial replacement can amplify the 1MB
`EDIT_MAX_FILE_BYTES` input cap into arbitrarily large output (e.g. 1MB file + 50-char replacement
per char → ~50MB). `validate_write_content` already exists in this file and is wired into
`tool_write_file` (line 1062), but not into `tool_edit_file`'s regex path — apply it (or an
analogous output cap) on `applied` before atomic_write to preserve the edit-file size contract.

                      .to_string(),
              ));
          }
+         // 防止 regex 多处匹配把输出膨胀过 EDIT_MAX_FILE_BYTES：与 write_file 对齐，复用 validate_write_content
+         validate_write_content(&applied).map_err(|e| {
+             (
+                 EditErrorKind::NotFound,
+                 format!("regex 替换结果超上限：{e}"),
+             )
+         })?;
          let delta = applied.lines().count() as i64 - content.lines().count() as i64;
          return Ok((applied, EditMode::Regex, delta));

─── src-tauri/src/py/document.rs:1525-1540 ───
[security · high] `pptx_template_resolve_in` directly joins the user-supplied template name into a
filesystem path: `dir.join(format!("{n}.pptx"))`. There is no rejection of path separators, `..`
segments, NUL bytes, or Windows device names — an LLM steered via prompt injection can pass
`template = "../builtin_templates/pptx-default"` (or with absolute components) and `.exists()` at
line 1538 will succeed for paths outside `pptx_templates/`. The newly added `template` parameter on
`SCHEMA_CREATE_PPT` exposes this surface to model input. Apply the same per-component whitelist used
by `create_skill_at` (alnum/`-`/`_`/`.` only, no `..`, no separators) and/or use
`Path::components()` to assert every component is `Normal` before joining.

[security · high] `word_template_resolve_in` has the same path-traversal issue as
`pptx_template_resolve_in` (line 1525-1540): `dir.join(format!("{n}.docx"))` with no name
validation. With the newly added `template` parameter on `SCHEMA_CREATE_WORD`, model-controlled
input flows here. Apply identical per-component whitelist. Note: the `default_marker_path(dir)`
lookup at line 1347 (`def = std::fs::read_to_string(default_marker_path(dir)).ok()?`) is also
untrusted-output driven but only used as a fallback when `name` is None, so impact is bounded —

─── src-tauri/src/bot_fs.rs:657-664 ───
[security · high] The `regex` mode path uses bare `Regex::new(old)` with no
`RegexBuilder::size_limit(...)` or `dfa_size_limit(...)` and no execution timeout. Adversarial regex
(e.g. nested quantifiers like `(a+)+$`) on the maximum 1MB file (`EDIT_MAX_FILE_BYTES`, line 30) can
lock the worker thread via catastrophic backtracking. While this runs on `spawn_blocking` (good —
doesn't block the async runtime), a single poisoned file still pins one blocking-pool thread
indefinitely. Add `RegexBuilder::size_limit(64 * 1024 * 1024).dfa_size_limit(64 * 1024 *
1024).build(old)` so ReDoS-style inputs fail fast at compile-time. Also consider bounding the input
length pre-regex beyond the 1MB read cap, since `find_iter().count()` (line 665) iterates the whole
content before replacement.

      if use_regex {
          use regex::Regex;
-         let re = match Regex::new(old) {
+         // size_limit/dfa_size_limit 防御 ReDoS：用户在 LLM 控制下可注入
+         // 嵌套量词模式，1MB 上限文件 + 病态模式可锁阻塞线程（spawn_blocking）
+         let re = match regex::RegexBuilder::new(old)
+             .size_limit(64 * 1024 * 1024)
+             .dfa_size_limit(64 * 1024 * 1024)
+             .build()
+         {
              Ok(r) => r,
              Err(e) => {
                  return Err((EditErrorKind::NotFound, format!("regex 模式串非法：{e}")));
              }
          };

[maintainability · medium] `fabricated_gen_path` only triggers when `tool_calls.is_empty()` (see
`bot_model_loop.rs` lines 1337-1371). After a successful `create_skill` tool call, an LLM can still
fabricate text like "已创建技能 foo" without the on-disk skill actually existing — the guard will not
fire because tool calls WERE made. Since `create_skill` has `claims_patterns: &[]` (line 983) and
writes to `.md` not `.docx/.xlsx/.pptx/.pdf`, `fabricated_gen_path` also won't catch it. Either: (a)
add skill-claim patterns like `已创建技能`/`技能已生效` to `create_skill.claims_patterns`, or (b) add an
extension-agnostic post-write verifier that confirms the SKILL.md exists at the claimed directory
when the model text claims skill creation. The same gap exists for `edit_file`/`write_file` text
claims when the tool actually returned Err — `claims_patterns` checks are bypassed if
`mutation_done` was set by ANY prior mutating tool in the same round.



─── src-tauri/src/bot/registry.rs:660-670 ───
[security · medium] The path-tokenizer has multiple Windows parsing holes: (a) `boundaries` (line
660-663) doesn't include `'\\'`, so a quoted Windows path like `"C:\Users\test user\report.docx"`
will be split at the space, yielding the truncated `C:\Users\test` (which doesn't match `.docx` and
is silently dropped — so the model can sneak fake Windows paths past the guard by putting a space in
them); (b) UNC paths `\\server\share\file.docx` start with `\\` so neither `tok.starts_with('/')`
nor `tok.as_bytes()[1] == b':'` matches (positions 1 is `\`); (c) the function returns the FIRST
non-existent path, so a model claiming "已生成 a.docx, b.docx, c.docx" where a/b exist and c doesn't
will still fire — but a model claiming one real file and one fake file returns the fake one
(acceptable), while a model claiming 3 fake files returns just the first (acceptable). Add `'\\'` to
boundaries AND a UNC-path detector (`tok.starts_with(r"\\")`) to handle Windows-shaped inputs from
Chinese LLMs.

+     // 分隔符加 `'\\'`（Windows 路径用反斜杠，与 `/` 同样作为边界）；
+     // UNC 路径以 `\\` 起头，在过滤阶段另开一支判断
      let boundaries: &[char] = &[
-         ' ', '\t', '\n', '\r', '`', '"', '\'', '「', '」', '（', '）', '(', ')', '，', '。', '；',
+         ' ', '\t', '\n', '\r', '\\', '`', '"', '\'', '「', '」', '（', '）', '(', ')', '，', '。', '；',
          '、', ',', ';', ':', '：', '　',
      ];
      text.split(boundaries)
          .map(|tok| tok.trim_matches(|c: char| matches!(c, '*' | '_' | '-' | '>' | '#')))
          .filter(|tok| {
              let low = tok.to_lowercase();
              DOC_EXTS.iter().any(|e| low.ends_with(e))
          })
-         .filter(|tok| tok.starts_with('/') || (tok.len() > 2 && tok.as_bytes()[1] == b':'))
+         .filter(|tok| {
+             tok.starts_with('/')
+                 || tok.starts_with(r"\\")  // UNC：\\server\share\file.docx
+                 || (tok.len() > 2 && tok.as_bytes()[1] == b':')
+         })

─── src-tauri/src/bot/registry.rs:979-986 ───
[security · high] `create_skill` is `mutating: true` but has `claims_patterns: &[]`. This means
`claims_mutation("已创建技能 foo")` returns false — the LLM can fabricate skill creation claims without
the claims-mutation guard catching them. This is a related but distinct gap from confirmed finding
#7: that one notes `fabricated_gen_path` only fires when `tool_calls.is_empty()`, while this gap
exists in the upstream `claims_mutation` filter regardless of tool_calls. The accompanying test
`every_mutating_tool_has_matching_claim_pattern` uses a hardcoded `samples` list that ends at
`("write_artifact_file", ...)`, so `create_skill` (and `edit_file`, `write_file`) slip through the
assertion `!t.claims_patterns.is_empty()`. Suggested fix: (a) populate `claims_patterns` for
`create_skill` (e.g. `&["已创建技能", "已新建技能", "已注册技能"]`), (b) make the test iterate over
`TOOLS_TABLE.iter().filter(|t| t.mutating)` instead of a hardcoded list so future mutating tools
can't bypass the invariant.

          name: "create_skill",

[bug · high] `materialize_one` 的幂等门 `existed && disk_version(...) == bundled_version(...)` 存在 TOCTOU
竞态与恢复漏洞：1) `dest.exists()` 与随后 `disk_version` 的 `read_to_string` 之间，文件可能被并发删除/截断/替换，导致 `existed`
失真——但 `write` 仍以失真的 `existed` 区分 `Seeded` vs `Updated`，审计分类会前后矛盾；2) 当 `disk_version == None`（磁盘读不到或
frontmatter 损坏）且 `bundled_version == None`（资产非 UTF-8）时，`None == None` 成立，本函数返回
`Unchanged`，损坏的磁盘文件**不会被自愈重写**——只有 `builtin_assets_parse_clean` 测试在保护，但若未来新增一个非 UTF-8 资产，recovery
路径就静默失效。建议把「disk 版本缺失」与「disk 已损坏」拆开：磁盘读不到 / 解析不出 frontmatter 时强制走 `Updated`（覆盖自愈），而不是依赖 `==`
比较的回退语义。

  fn materialize_one(dir: &std::path::Path, skill: &BuiltinSkill) -> SeedOutcome {
      let dest_dir = dir.join(skill.dir_name);
      let dest = dest_dir.join("SKILL.md");
-     let existed = dest.exists();
-     if existed && disk_version(&dest, skill.dir_name) == bundled_version(skill) {
+     let disk = disk_version(&dest, skill.dir_name);
+     let bundled = bundled_version(skill);
+     // 磁盘副本读不到 / 解析不出 frontmatter → 视为损坏，强制重写自愈；
+     // 即便 bundled 也是 None，也按 Updated 处理（保证「坏 → 好」始终发生，
+     // 不依赖 Option 相等的隐式语义）
+     let needs_write = match (&disk, &bundled) {
+         (_, None) => dest.exists(),             // bundled 资产不可解析，必须重写
+         (None, _) => true,                      // 磁盘损坏，必须自愈
+         (Some(d), Some(b)) => d != b,           // 版本差异，按需覆盖
+     };
+     if !needs_write {
          return SeedOutcome::Unchanged;
      }
+     let existed = dest.exists();


─── src-tauri/src/bot_skills/builtin.rs:14-17 ───
[security · medium] `BuiltinSkill` 是 `pub` 结构体，`dir_name: &'static str` 是 `pub` 字段，路径直接通过
`dir.join(skill.dir_name)` 拼接，**没有任何字符集校验或规范化**。当前 `BUILTIN_SKILLS` 是硬编码常量所以安全，但 API 表面上允许任意构造（含
`..`、绝对路径成分、非法 Windows 名字符），任何未来从插件/注册表/远程源动态装载 `BuiltinSkill` 的代码都会静默获得路径穿越能力。建议加一个内部 `pub(crate)
fn new(dir_name, content)` 构造器强制 `dir_name` 通过 `SKILL_NAME_CHARS_OK`（与 `manage::validate_skill_name`
同口径），并把字段降到 `pub(crate)`，或在 `materialize_one` 顶部对 `skill.dir_name` 做防御性断言。这样即使 API
被外部误用，跨越意图在构造时就被拒。

  pub struct BuiltinSkill {
-     pub dir_name: &'static str,
-     pub content: &'static [u8],
+     dir_name: &'static str,
+     content: &'static [u8],
+ }
+ 
+ impl BuiltinSkill {
+     pub const fn new(dir_name: &'static str, content: &'static [u8]) -> Self {
+         // 字符集与 manage::validate_skill_name / parse::SKILL_NAME_CHARS_OK 同步：
+         // ASCII 字母数字 + `-` / `_`。空串 / 含 `..` / 含路径分隔符在此编译期拒掉，
+         // 防止任何动态构造者（注册表 / 远程源 / 测试 fixture）悄悄塞进穿越 segment。
+         let bytes = dir_name.as_bytes();
+         let mut i = 0;
+         while i < bytes.len() {
+             let b = bytes[i];
+             let ok = b.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || b == b'-' || b == b'_';
+             if !ok {
+                 panic!("BuiltinSkill::new: dir_name \"{}\" 字符集非法（仅允许字母/数字/-/_）", dir_name);
+             }
+             i += 1;
+         }
+         assert!(!bytes.is_empty(), "BuiltinSkill::new: dir_name 不得为空");
+         Self { dir_name, content }
+     }
+ 
+     pub fn dir_name(&self) -> &'static str { self.dir_name }
+     pub fn content(&self) -> &'static [u8] { self.content }
  }

SCHED_TICK_SECS），注释说「防漂移」。但反过来也意味着：如果未来任何原因把 tick 调大（节电/限频），宽限会同步变长——例如把 tick 调到 5 分钟，宽限就是 10

[bug · medium] Over-restrictive `p.contains("..")` rejects legitimate filenames. A file named
`data..backup.txt`, `report..final.md`, or `version..1/sub.txt` contains the literal substring `..`
but is otherwise a fully valid relative path the per-component `Component::Normal` walk below would
accept. The substring check fires before the per-component check, so authors can't ship
self-describing filenames that the file system routinely permits. Drop the redundant substring guard
(or move it strictly to detect traversal segments via a regex on the components), and let the
component-level validation be the source of truth.

Suggested fix: drop `p.contains("..")` entirely; rely on the `Component::Normal` walk + `s == ".."`
rejection below (or, if you want belt-and-suspenders, also reject any path whose components
stringify to literal `..`).

              });
          }
+         // 逐组件校验：精确拒绝 `..` 穿越（不依赖子串启发式，免得误杀 `data..backup.txt`）

[bug · medium] Race-coded-as-exists: `std::fs::create_dir(&dest)` collapses every I/O failure into
`SkillLoadFailed { reason: "同名技能已存在：如需覆盖请先删除" }`. The only error variant this message accurately
describes is `AlreadyExists` from a concurrent double-create; other real causes (parent directory
vanished, ACL denied on Windows, `dest` is a stale non-directory file left by a previous failed
import) become indistinguishable user-facing "exists" errors. Match on the actual
`io::ErrorKind::AlreadyExists` and route other failures to `IoError` so reports and retry logic can
act on the real cause.

This is also the standard pattern used in `skills_import`; the new `create_skill_at` should be at
least as precise as the canonical reference.

+             return Err(CommandError::SkillLoadFailed {

[maintainability · low] Mixed units in size accounting: `CREATE_MAX_SKILL_CHARS = 64 * 1024` is
enforced on `content.chars().count()` (Unicode scalar values) while `CREATE_MAX_TOTAL_BYTES` is
accumulated against `content.len()` (UTF-8 byte count) plus per-attachment `c.len()`. A doc with a
50 K-char CJK body can pass the char cap while still being 150 KB on disk, and a future maintainer
tightening the byte cap to 64 KiB while reading the "字符" comment will silently break. Either change
`CREATE_MAX_SKILL_CHARS` to a byte cap and rename the constant, or accumulate
`content.chars().count() + sum(c.chars().count())` so both checks speak the same units.

-     if content.chars().count() > CREATE_MAX_SKILL_CHARS {
+     if content.len() > CREATE_MAX_SKILL_BYTES {

+             reason: format!("SKILL.md 超过 {CREATE_MAX_SKILL_BYTES} 字节上限"),

─── src-tauri/src/bot_skills/vars.rs:6-11 ───
[bug · high] The new `[0-9a-f]{32}` alternative is too permissive and creates false positives. It
will match any 32-char lowercase hex string — not just UUIDs. Since `TASK_ID_RE.find()` returns the
leftmost match, a non-UUID hex string that appears earlier in the tool result (e.g., MD5 hash,
SHA-256 prefix, cache key, debug hex dump, session/token id, file content hash) will shadow a real
task UUID written later in the same output. The previous 36-char hyphenated UUID was over-strict;
this fix over-corrects and trades one failure mode for another.

Concrete risky scenario — a tool that returns both:
  "DEBUG hash=deadbeefdeadbeefdeadbeefdeadbeef task_id=7c9e6679-7425-40de-944b-e07fc1f90ae7"
Old regex: extracts `7c9e6679-7425-40de-944b-e07fc1f90ae7` (correct).
New regex: extracts `deadbeefdeadbeefdeadbeefdeadbeef` and `${stepN.id}` will then point at a
non-existent / wrong task — silent data corruption in downstream tools.

The doc comment still calls this a "UUID" regex, which is no longer accurate; the function now
extracts "any 32-char hex string" and the function name `extract_task_id` becomes a misnomer.

Suggested hardening: require the hex string to be UUID v4-shaped (version nibble `4` at position 12,
variant nibble in `[89ab]` at position 16), or require an enclosing context (preceded by a UUID-like
prefix like `id=`, `"id":"`, `task id:`), or restrict to
`[0-9a-f]{8}[0-9a-f]{4}4[0-9a-f]{3}[89ab][0-9a-f]{15}[0-9a-f]{8}` (32-hex UUID-v4 simple form). At
minimum, add a regression test that asserts a 32-hex hash appearing before a hyphenated UUID does
NOT shadow the UUID.

[bug · medium] `std::fs::write` 直接覆盖目标文件，不是原子操作。若进程在写入中途被杀（OOM、用户强杀、电源断电、磁盘满到一半），SKILL.md 可能被截断到 0
字节或半截 YAML，下次启动 `disk_version` 会返回 `None`（空文件读不到
frontmatter），触发再次覆盖自愈——但中间这段「文件已存在但内容损坏」的窗口里，技能被加载时（`scan_skill_dirs` 在 materialize
同帧/后续路径里读）会拿到坏内容并写进路由缓存。改用 `tempfile::NamedTempFile::new_in(&dest_dir)` + `persist` 走 rename
原子替换，确保读者要么看到旧版要么看到完整新版，看不到半截。

-         Ok(()) => SeedOutcome::Seeded,

+         Ok(true) if existed => SeedOutcome::Updated,
+         Ok(_) => SeedOutcome::Seeded,
          Err(e) => SeedOutcome::Failed(format!("write: {e}")),
+     }
+ 
+ fn write_atomic(dest: &std::path::Path, content: &[u8]) -> std::io::Result<bool> {
+     let parent = dest.parent().ok_or_else(|| {
+         std::io::Error::new(std::io::ErrorKind::InvalidInput, "dest 缺父目录")
+     })?;
+     let mut tmp = tempfile::NamedTempFile::new_in(parent)?;
+     use std::io::Write as _;
+     tmp.write_all(content)?;
+     tmp.as_file().sync_all()?;
+     tmp.persist(dest).map_err(|e| e.error)?;
+     Ok(true)
-     }
+ }

`Option::None`。注释说「正确性由 builtin_assets_parse_clean 测试锁死」——但这只覆盖 CI 构建期。若某次 release 把非 UTF-8
资源（误把二进制文件 include_bytes! 进来、构建期编码异常）打入生产，启动期 `bundled_version == None`，与磁盘版本比较时：磁盘已被上次启动物化为同名坏字节 →
`disk_version == None` → `None == None` → `Unchanged`，坏文件永远不被自愈；磁盘还没物化 → `disk_version` 返回
`Some(前一次发布的合法版本)` → `Some == None` → `Updated` 把磁盘上**用户本地修改过的版本**覆盖回坏字节（用户数据丢失）。把 `unwrap_or("")`
换成显式 panic 或在 `materialize_at_startup` 顶部一次性 `from_utf8(...).expect("内置资产必须

+     // 一次性 panic（避免 None == None / Some == None 两类分支把破损状态永久埋住）
+     let text = std::str::from_utf8(skill.content).expect("内置资产须为合法 UTF-8");
      parse_meta(text, skill.dir_name).version

[maintainability · low] All error paths in `tool_create_skill` are funneled through `ToolResult::ok`
with a free-form Chinese string, with three different inline comments justifying why each message
starts with a non-error/warn prefix (e.g. `// 「create_skill 缺少 name」首字「c」非 error/warn 前缀 → ok`).
This is a fragile classifier convention: any future change that starts a failure message with
`E`、`e`、`W`、`w` (or rerouting through a localization layer that doesn't preserve the first
character) silently turns a failure into a user-visible success. Use `ToolResult::err` with
structured payload for every failure path, and let `ok` carry only true success payloads. If you
need to keep the same conversation shape, define a marker explicit in the body (e.g.
`ok=true/false`) instead of relying on prefix sniffing downstream.

+         return ToolResult::err(
              "create_skill 缺少 name（技能名：字母/数字/-/_）".to_string(),
              Vec::new(),
          );
      };

[bug · critical] Frontmatter name vs directory name mismatch creates an unreachable skill. The
user-supplied `name` argument becomes the directory under `skills_dir()`, but `parse_meta(content,
name)` may set `meta.name` to the frontmatter `name:` field (a different valid ASCII identifier).
After the create, `intent_rules_from_dirs` emits `IntentRule { skill_name: meta.name, .. }` and
`scan_skill_dirs` returns `SkillInfo { name: meta.name, .. }`; subsequent `use_skill` calls and the
settings-page delete button both resolve the skill by the directory name, so an intent-matched
dispatch lands on a non-existent directory and reports "技能「<frontmatter-name>」不存在" — the bot cannot
use the skill it just created. Enforce `meta.name == name` (or strip the `name:` field from
`content` and re-emit it from the `name` argument) before accepting the create.

  let meta = parse_meta(content, name);
      validate_created_meta(&meta).map_err(|reason| CommandError::InvalidArgument {
          field: "content".into(),
          value: String::new(),
          reason,

+     // 强制约定：frontmatter `name:` 必须等于目录名（参数 `name`）。否则路由到的
+     // `skill_name` 是 frontmatter 名，但 on-disk 目录是参数名 → use_skill 找不到技能。
+     // skills_import 路径不会撞这条（目录名 = frontmatter 名 = parse_frontmatter 结果），
+     // 这里两条独立通道必须收敛。
+     if meta.name != name {
+         return Err(CommandError::InvalidArgument {
+             field: "content".into(),
+             value: String::new(),

[maintainability · medium] `eprintln!("[db] app_data_dir 解析失败，跳过 legacy db 拷贝：{e}")` breaks the
local convention. The sibling legacy-DB copy path immediately above uses
`crate::audit::write_event(..., AuditLevel::Warn/Error, "legacy_db_copy", ...)` for both success and
failure telemetry. The established `eprintln!` convention in this module is reserved for
`[mutex_poisoned]` poison-recovery lines (see `lock_db_write` and the `LEGACY_COPY_LOCK` poison
handler a few lines above). Switching to `eprintln!` here means this specific failure mode will not
appear in `bot.log` or the in-app audit panel — the very issue the repo's audit-first convention was
built to avoid (per `AUDIT-REPORT-MANUAL-2026-08-18.md` "raw `eprintln!` 走 stderr，不会进 bot.log
审计文件"). Route this through `crate::audit::write_event(app, crate::audit::AuditLevel::Warn,
"legacy_db_copy", &[("error", e.to_string())])` so legacy-DB failures are consistently observable.

                  Err(e) => {
-                     eprintln!("[db] app_data_dir 解析失败，跳过 legacy db 拷贝：{e}");
+                     crate::audit::write_event(

[bug · low] Control character filter is incomplete: `\x7F` (DEL) is not in the excluded set, so a
body containing DEL will pass through and can still corrupt terminal-style consumers (logs that pipe
through less, IDE terminals, etc.). The stated intent ("防 toast/日志串行") is broader than what this
filter catches. Add `'\x7F'` to the excluded patterns, e.g. `\x00`..=`\x08` | `\x0B` | `\x0C` |
`\x0E`..=`\x1F` | `\x7F`. Also consider extending to C1 controls (`\u{80}`..=`\u{9F}`) if upstream
payloads may include raw Latin-1 dirty bytes. Additionally, the new sanitization has no test
coverage — recommend adding a unit test that constructs a `CommandError::LlmApiError` with a body
containing `\x00`, `\x01`, `\x07`, `\x7F`, `\n`, `\t` and asserts that only the printable / \t\n\r
characters survive in `message()`.

  Self::LlmApiError {
                  status,
                  body_preview,
              } => {
                  // 剥控制字符（保留 \t\n\r）：上游错误体可能混入脏字节，防 toast/日志串行
+                 // 注意：也剥 \x7F (DEL) —— 终端/IDE 日志会把前一个字符吞掉
                  format!(
                      "大模型 API 错误 {status}：{}",
                      body_preview
                          .chars()
                          .filter(|c| !matches!(
                              c,
-                             '\x00'..='\x08' | '\x0B' | '\x0C' | '\x0E'..='\x1F'
+                             '\x00'..='\x08'
+                                 | '\x0B'
+                                 | '\x0C'
+                                 | '\x0E'..='\x1F'
+                                 | '\x7F'
                          ))
                          .collect::<String>()
                  )
              }


─── src-tauri/src/db/workflow_audit.rs:140-142 ───
[maintainability · medium] docstring 对「保留策略兜底」的描述与 `wa_prune` 的实际语义不符：`wa_prune` 按 `run_started_at`
全局裁剪（`SELECT DISTINCT run_started_at … LIMIT ?1`），而不是按 `(workflow_id, run_started_at)` 复合键
per-workflow 裁剪——多个活跃工作流同时写入时，单个工作流能保留的 run 数会被全局池稀释，最坏情况可能只剩个位数 run 而不是「5..=100 个」。当导出文件长期稳定大于 UI
列表的 500 钳制时，调试者容易按本注释预期去查保留策略、误判是 prune
没跑，进而错过「同一时间窗其他工作流挤占预算」这一根因。建议改成「体量由保留策略兜底（保留是全局跨工作流的，单工作流实际可保留 run 数 ≤
audit_retention_runs，且取决于同时活跃的其他工作流）」，或者把 `wa_prune` 改成 per-(workflow_id, run_started_at)
复合键后再写「per-workflow 5..=100」措辞。

`wa_export_list(&conn, &wid2).map_err(crate::error::CommandError::DbError)?`，意味着函数返回
`Result<Vec<AuditEntry>, CommandError>` 就能让调用方直接 `?` 透传，省去 `.map_err` 再转一次，且底层 rusqlite

[test · low] 测试夹具与本注释描述的真实失败形态脱节：`1000 + i` 让 505 行每一行都是独立 run（共 505 个 run，每个 1 条事件），实际工程里触发原 bug
的形态是「单 run 多节点事件 > 500」——前者无论如何裁剪都不会踩到 `wa_list` 的 500 行单 run 钳制（因为单个 run 的事件量始终为 1），所以这条断言只验证了「不按
LIMIT 钳 505 行」这个弱性质，没真正覆盖 docstring 里说的「多 run × 多节点事件可超 500 条」场景。建议补一个 fixture：固定 `run_started_at =
1000`，插入 600 条 `KIND_NODE_START`，断言 `wa_export_list` 全量返回 600 条，这样任何未来的「按 run 二次裁剪」回归都会被立即打中。

─── src-tauri/src/db/workflow_settings.rs:128-135 ───
[maintainability · low] After `tx.commit()` the function reads via `settings_view(conn)` (outer
connection) rather than via `&tx`. This works because `unchecked_transaction` defaults to DEFERRED
and writes are visible on the same connection post-commit, but the function mixes `&tx` for writes
and `conn` for the final read after commit, which is mildly inconsistent. A cleaner shape is to read
inside the transaction scope and commit afterward, so the entire mutation+read path stays on one
handle and the read cannot accidentally observe a concurrent writer on a shared connection.

─── src-tauri/src/evolution/derive.rs:196-200 ───
[bug · medium] The guard `2 >= t.contradiction_min_ids` hardcodes the LHS operand count instead of
deriving it from the `Contradiction` op. The field name (`min_ids`) and the doc table heading
(`ids.len ≥ 1`) both imply a count comparison, but the implementation freezes the LHS at `2` because
`Contradiction { keep, drop_id, content }` always happens to involve exactly two ids.

Concrete consequences:
- A user who reads the code expecting `ids.len >= t.contradiction_min_ids` will be surprised when
`contradiction_min_ids: 2` produces a proposal (it does, because `2 >= 2` is true), even though a
`Contradiction` op has exactly 2 ids and is at the boundary.
- The "set ≥3 ≈ off" semantics rely entirely on the assumption `Contradiction` always has exactly 2
ids. If the variant ever grows a context id, switches to a `Vec<String>`, or ever produces a
single-id contradiction, the threshold silently stops gating anything and the user-facing knob
becomes meaningless.
- The setting is documented as a 4-档 advisory in `EvolutionPanel` (`DERIVE_THRESHOLD_META` mirrors
`1..=99`), giving users the impression they're tuning a real count-based predicate.

Either:
1. Compute the count explicitly (`let involved = match op { ConsolidateOp::Contradiction { keep,
drop_id, .. } => if keep.is_empty() || drop_id.is_empty() { 0 } else { 2 }, ... }` or similar) and
compare `involved >= t.contradiction_min_ids` — making the contract what the field name advertises;
or
2. Replace the field with a truthful `enable_contradiction_proposals: bool` (off/on) since the count
is invariant.

          ConsolidateOp::Contradiction {
              keep,
              drop_id,
              content,
-         } if 2 >= t.contradiction_min_ids => {
+         } if contradiction_count(op) >= t.contradiction_min_ids => {
+ 
+ // 稳定地表达 contradiction 涉及的条目数（当前类型 keep+drop = 2；
+ // 未来若结构变，此处是单一不变量的边界守卫，不分散到每一个 caller）。
+ fn contradiction_count(_op: &ConsolidateOp) -> usize { 2 }

[bug · medium] `derive_lesson_proposal` ignores `DeriveThresholds` entirely while producing a
`Medium`-impact proposal — the same impact tier that `auto_apply_gate` admits. At the single call
site today (`evolution/mod.rs::post_consolidation`), the lesson proposal is appended to the
`proposals` Vec *after* the threshold-gated ops are derived, so a user who has set `merge_min_ids:
99` (i.e., "effectively turn off ops-derived proposals") still receives and may auto-apply lesson
proposals every reflection round.

This breaks the implicit mental model the Settings UI advertises with the card "提案派生门槛" — users
reasonably expect the threshold card to gate the full proposal stream, not just the ops slice of it.

Either:
- Pass `&DeriveThresholds` (or a separate `enable_lessons: bool`) into `derive_lesson_proposal` and
gate emission on it; or
- Document explicitly at the call site that lesson proposals are exempt from `DeriveThresholds`, and
add a corresponding control to `EvolutionPanel` so users can opt out.

[maintainability · low] The doc comment on `DeriveThresholds` states "读取/落盘经 `clamped`" (read/write
goes through `clamped`), but `derive_proposals_with` accepts the raw `&DeriveThresholds` and trusts
the caller to have already sanitised it. The only caller today is
`evolution/mod.rs::post_consolidation`, which goes through `policy::read_derive_thresholds` (which
clamps), so it's safe in practice. However the doc contract will silently break on the next caller:

- A future Tauri command or test that constructs `DeriveThresholds { merge_min_ids: 0,
..Default::default() }` and feeds it to `derive_proposals_with` will emit proposals with
`occurrence_count = 0` (empty `ids` qualifies because `0 >= 0` is true), violating the "异常 ids 全安全"
semantics the tests claim to prove.
- `set_derive_thresholds_at` also clamps, so the only path that bypasses it is direct construction —
easy to miss in a future refactor that splits the read/write paths.

Either clamp internally on entry (defence-in-depth: `let t = t.clamped();` as the first line of
`derive_proposals_with`), or change the doc comment to be explicit about the caller's
responsibility.

─── src-tauri/src/evolution/policy.rs:128-132 ───
[maintainability · medium] Asymmetric IO error handling between sibling readers.
`read_apply_policy_at` (lines 41-51) was rewritten in this diff to differentiate `NotFound` (silent)
from other IO errors (logged via `eprintln!`), with a comment explaining that silent bypass of other
errors would defeat governance diagnostics. The newly-added sibling `read_derive_thresholds_at`
(lines 130-132) regresses to the original silent-swallow pattern — `let Ok(raw) =
std::fs::read_to_string(path) else { return DeriveThresholds::default(); }` swallows every IO error,
including transient PermissionDenied/IO faults. This is reachable from `post_consolidation` every
consolidate round (see `evolution/mod.rs:168`), so a transient read failure silently downgrades the
user-configured thresholds to defaults with no diagnostic. Mirror the `read_apply_policy_at` shape:
emit one stderr line for non-NotFound errors and only swallow `NotFound` silently as the
"unconfigured" path.

Suggested fix:
    let raw = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {
        Ok(r) => r,
        Err(e) => {
            if e.kind() != std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound {
                eprintln!("[evolution] 读 {path:?} 失败（{e}），派生门槛按默认放行");
            }
            return DeriveThresholds::default();
        }
    };

  pub fn read_derive_thresholds_at(path: &Path) -> crate::evolution::derive::DeriveThresholds {
      use crate::evolution::derive::DeriveThresholds;
-     let Ok(raw) = std::fs::read_to_string(path) else {
+     let raw = match std::fs::read_to_string(path) {
+         Ok(r) => r,
+         Err(e) => {
+             if e.kind() != std::io::ErrorKind::NotFound {
+                 eprintln!("[evolution] 读 {path:?} 失败（{e}），派生门槛按默认放行");
+             }
-         return DeriveThresholds::default();
+             return DeriveThresholds::default();
+         }
      };

[maintainability · low] Duplicated RMW setup. `set_apply_policy_at` (lines 88-115) and the new
`set_derive_thresholds_at` (lines 148-179) share the same lock-acquire + read-file +
parse-or-recover + mutate-mutation-block + atomic_write plumbing, with only the inserted key/value
differing. The duplicated bodies (15+ identical lines each) will drift (one gains a new check, the
other forgets), and the failure-mode contract relies on the duplicates staying in lock-step. Extract
a helper such as `mutate_evolution_block(path, key, value)` that owns the lock, the parse-or-bail,
the object/evolution-block navigation, and the atomic_write — both setters reduce to one call. This
also centralizes the `unwrap_or_else(|e| { ...; e.into_inner() })` mutex-poison dance, which is
currently copy-pasted twice.

─── src-tauri/src/evolution/panel/commands.rs:886-895 ───
[performance · medium] Block on synchronous IO without `spawn_blocking`. `evolution_get_thresholds`
and `evolution_set_thresholds` (declared in `evolution/panel/commands.rs:886-895`) are `pub fn`
(sync) `#[tauri::command]` handlers that call `read_derive_thresholds` / `set_derive_thresholds` —
both perform blocking filesystem I/O on the Tauri runtime thread. Every other evolution_* command in
that file wraps blocking work in `crate::py::document::spawn_blocking_map` (e.g.
`evolution_get_apply_policy` at line 752, `evolution_set_apply_policy` at line 758). The new pair is
the only outlier and will stall the tokio worker on every invocation. Declare them `pub async fn`
and route through `spawn_blocking_map` for symmetry with the sibling `apply_policy` commands.

Suggested fix (commands.rs):
    #[tauri::command]
    pub async fn evolution_get_thresholds(app: AppHandle) -> Result<DeriveThresholds, String> {
        crate::py::document::spawn_blocking_map(move || Ok(read_derive_thresholds(&app))).await
    }

    #[tauri::command]
    pub async fn evolution_set_thresholds(app: AppHandle, thresholds: DeriveThresholds) ->
Result<(), String> {
        crate::py::document::spawn_blocking_map(move || set_derive_thresholds(&app,
thresholds)).await
    }

      }

[security · low] The format-injection fix is half-completed for defense-in-depth purposes.
`m.content` (column 4) is sanitized — good — but `m.id` (column 1) is inserted unchecked into the
same `{} | {} | {} | {}` row. Today all live ids are `uuid::Uuid::new_v4().simple().to_string()`

arbitrary text into `MemItem.id` re-opens the alignment-shift path this fix is closing. Either
extend the same `replace('|', "｜")` (and `\n`/`\r` flatten) to `m.id`, or assert id shape at the
format boundary (e.g. `debug_assert!(!m.id.contains('|') && !m.id.contains('\n'))`). `importance:
i64` is fine; `m.kind` is free-form `String` but constrained by document convention — flagging only
for completeness.

[maintainability · low] The 400-char cap on `parse_lesson` is a magic number with no justification
comment, and the truncation is silent — downstream (audit / `derive::derive_lesson_proposal`) cannot
distinguish "lesson was 380 chars" from "lesson was 800 chars and lost the last 400". Two issues
bundled:
1. Explain why 400 — is it a content-length ceiling matching the reflection column
(`kind=reflection` cap), or an arbitrary token budget?
2. Either return a structured result like `Lesson { content, truncated: bool }` so callers can
audit/log when truncation fires, or pair the silent truncation with a `tracing::debug!(...
"truncated chars" = N)` at minimum. As-is, behavior rules beyond the cap disappear with no
observable side effect.

- pub fn parse_lesson(text: &str) -> Option<String> {
+ const LESSON_MAX_CHARS: usize = 400;
+ 
+ pub struct ParsedLesson {
+     pub content: String,
+     /// 原始文本超过 `LESSON_MAX_CHARS` 时为 true——下游可记审计或拒绝落盘。
+     pub truncated: bool,
+ }
+ 
+ pub fn parse_lesson(text: &str) -> Option<ParsedLesson> {
      let v = extract_json(text)?;
      let raw = match v.get("lesson") {
          Some(serde_json::Value::String(s)) => s.clone(),
          Some(serde_json::Value::Object(o)) => o.get("content")?.as_str()?.to_string(),
          _ => return None,
      };
      let t = raw.trim();
      if t.is_empty() {
          return None;
      }
-     Some(t.chars().take(400).collect())
+     let truncated = t.chars().count() > LESSON_MAX_CHARS;
+     let content: String = t.chars().take(LESSON_MAX_CHARS).collect();
+     Some(ParsedLesson { content, truncated })
  }

+ fn derive_lesson_proposal_basic_contract() {
+     let now_ms = chrono::Utc::now().timestamp_millis();

[other · medium] The `WHERE state = 'pending'` guard is the SOLE defense against double-run for
`recovery.rs` callers (which bypass `db_write_lock()` and call `_inner` directly via their own
connection). The wrapper `journal_committed` adds the lock as a second line of defense, but
production `recovery::journal_replay_pending` does not take it — so this SQL clause must not be
weakened under the impression that the lock makes it redundant. Consider rewording the rationale to
call out that `_inner` is lock-free on the recovery path and the guard is the invariant there.

[bug · medium] Two bool flags (`ok`, `stopped`) plus an `Option<String> err` are a textbook case for
invalid-state-representability. With three booleans-shaped-as-flags, calling sites can pass
`(ok=true, stopped=true, err=Some(..))` and the precedence order silently picks `stopped`; or
`(ok=false, stopped=false, err=None)` records a successful failure with no error message. Replace
with a `TraceOutcome` enum (`Done | Stopped | Timeout | Failed { err: Option<String> }`) at the
public API; the `stopped: bool` parameter is the kind of stringly/booleanly typed contract the
repo's type-design rule warns against. The OCR triage already proposed exactly this enum.

+ pub(crate) enum TraceOutcome {
+     Done,
+     Stopped,
+     Timeout,
+     Failed { err: Option<String> },
+ }
+

`trace_finish` (which derives `files_changed` from a `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM file_changes WHERE

`trace_finish` commits, so the aggregate count is permanently under-reported and the late inserts
become orphan rows (no FK relationship to the just-finished trace summary). Drain the writer before
removing the registry entry, or move the registry removal until after `trace_finish` succeeds. The

─── src-tauri/src/memory/extract.rs:438-444 ───
[bug · high] Demoting a duplicate in-batch `Update` to `New` does not actually prevent content loss
and instead breaks the `Update` path's "改口不改档" invariant via a different code path: `apply_insert` →
`insert_item_with` → `load_all` re-reads the just-updated row with the first fact's embedding. If
the second fact's embedding is within `dedup_merge_cosine` (default 0.92) of that, the merge branch
executes `UPDATE mem_items SET content=?, tags=?, importance=?, source=? WHERE id=?` on the same
row, overwriting (a) the first fact's content with the second fact's, and (b) the original row's
`tags`/`source`/`importance`/`kind` with the new item's defaults (`source="model_inferred"`,
`tags=""`). Net effect: the data loss the demotion was meant to avoid still happens (now via merge
instead of update), and the row's provenance (user_stated vs model_inferred, key tags, pinned
importance) is also clobbered. Safer alternatives: demote to `Skip` (silent drop, content may be
lost but provenance preserved), or have `apply_insert`/`insert_item_with` refuse to merge when the
merge target id is in the `claimed` set from this batch.

(user profile, app data). Either drop the chmod step here (consistent with "do nothing if we can't
lock down") or, preferably, mirror audit's `eprintln!` so that a chmod failure on this fallback path
is at least diagnosable — keeping the inline WARN honest about what just succeeded.

propagating as an unexpected type, or a future addition that `.unwrap()`s something) is silently
swallowed by `std::thread`'s default handler — exactly the diagnostic the WARN is meant to produce
disappears; (2) the fallback path is most likely hit on first launch / after upgrade / immediately

[other · medium] The spawn thread bypasses `BOT_LOG_LOCK` (audit.rs:338) on purpose — the
surrounding comment explains why (caller may hold the lock, std Mutex isn't reentrant) — but the
consequence is that this `write_fallback_warn` write can interleave byte-for-byte with main-thread
`audit::write_event` writes that DO hold `BOT_LOG_LOCK`. On Unix, `File::write_all` is not
guaranteed to be atomic past a single small `write(2)` syscall, so two concurrent appenders can
produce a torn line. This is rare (only first probe + fallback), but the data corruption lands in
the exact file the audit module is designed to keep clean — and `BOT_LOG_LOCK`'s existence is
precisely so this can't happen. Three reasonable fixes: (a) acquire `BOT_LOG_LOCK` from the spawned
thread (different thread → no deadlock risk, only blocks the fallback WARN briefly); (b) route
through `audit::write_event` once we re-enter a non-callback context; or (c) at minimum document the
deliberate bypass and the resulting interleaving risk, so a future maintainer doesn't "fix" it by
adding a recursive re-lock and create an actual deadlock.

[security · critical] Path traversal on user-controlled `name`: `dir.join(format!("{name}.docx"))`
with `name = "../../foo"` resolves to `data_dir/foo.docx`. With more `..`, escapes `data_dir`
entirely and can delete arbitrary `.docx` files. Validate name with an allow-list (e.g.
`name.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c=='-'||c=='_')`), then canonicalize the joined
path and assert it `starts_with(&dir)` before any fs op.

  pub fn word_template_delete(app: AppHandle, name: String) -> CommandResult<()> {
      let dir = word_templates_dir(&app);
+     // 拒绝含路径分隔符/.. 的 name；只用 file_stem 形态拼接
+     if name.is_empty() || name.contains('/') || name.contains('\\') || name.contains("..") {
+         return Err(CommandError::InvalidArgument {
+             field: "name".into(),
+             value: name,
+             reason: "模板名非法".into(),
+         });
+     }
      let p = dir.join(format!("{name}.docx"));
+     let canon = std::fs::canonicalize(&p).ok();
+     let allowed = std::fs::canonicalize(&dir).unwrap_or_else(|_| dir.clone());
+     if let Some(ref c) = canon {
+         if !c.starts_with(&allowed) {
+             return Err(CommandError::InvalidArgument {
+                 field: "name".into(),
+                 value: name,
+                 reason: "模板路径越界".into(),
+             });
+         }
+     }
      if p.exists() {
          std::fs::remove_file(&p).map_err(|e| CommandError::IoError(e.to_string()))?;
      }

─── src-tauri/src/py/document.rs:1506-1517 ───
[bug · low] `builtin_pptx_master_in` reads the cache, compares bytes, then conditionally writes —
non-atomic. Two concurrent first-callers can race; both will read "stale", both will write identical
bytes, the last writer's mtime is what wins. Today the bytes are identical so it's benign, but
`stale` will silently lose any future per-process cache-stamp change. Wrap the read-compare-write in
a single `if !cached_or_matches { write }` after acquiring a file lock or use `atomicwrites`.

  fn builtin_pptx_master_in(dir: &std::path::Path) -> CommandResult<String> {
      std::fs::create_dir_all(dir).map_err(|e| CommandError::IoError(e.to_string()))?;
      let dest = dir.join("pptx-default.pptx");
-     let stale = std::fs::read(&dest)
-         .map(|bytes| bytes != BUILTIN_PPTX_MASTER)
-         .unwrap_or(true);
-     if stale {
-         std::fs::write(&dest, BUILTIN_PPTX_MASTER)
+     // 原子化：write to temp + rename，避免并发首次调用出现短暂空文件
+     if std::fs::read(&dest).ok().as_deref() != Some(BUILTIN_PPTX_MASTER) {
+         let tmp = dir.join("pptx-default.pptx.tmp");
+         std::fs::write(&tmp, BUILTIN_PPTX_MASTER)
+             .map_err(|e| CommandError::IoError(e.to_string()))?;
+         std::fs::rename(&tmp, &dest)
              .map_err(|e| CommandError::IoError(e.to_string()))?;
      }
      Ok(dest.to_string_lossy().to_string())
  }

─── src/components/ChatPanel/ChatPanel.tsx:301-315 ───
[bug · high] Race condition: `setSessionId(cur.id)` (line 303) is not synchronously mirrored to
`sessionIdRef.current`, unlike the `switchSession`/`openExecSession`/`deleteSession` patterns. Until
the next render fires the `useEffect([sessionId])` mirror at lines 229-231, any
`bot-chat-delta`/`bot-tool`/`bot-usage-delta` arriving for the freshly-loaded session will be
filtered out by the `sessionIdRef.current !== sid` checks in the listeners, dropping the start of
the assistant's reply.

This is especially problematic because mount-time init is precisely when an in-flight streamed
continuation of the most-recent session is most likely to arrive (the user's previous session was
just loaded by `bot_history_load`, so any active stream continues into it).

Mirror the same pattern that was just added to `deleteSession`:
```ts
setSessionId(cur.id);
sessionIdRef.current = cur.id; // 同步镜像（与 switchSession/openExecSession/deleteSession 一致）
const rows = await invoke<...>("bot_history_load", { sessionId: cur.id });
if (rows?.length && sessionIdRef.current === cur.id) {
  setMessages(rowsToMsgs(rows));
}
```

Note: this same class of bug was flagged by the OCR scan on 2026-10-09
(`docs/OCR-SCAN-TRIAGE-2026-10-09.md` row "high · ChatPanel.tsx:301") and the equivalent suggestion
is in `docs/archive/process-2026-10/OCR-SCAN-FULL-2026-10-09.json`. The current PR only fixed the
`deleteSession` branch (the sibling bug at `ChatPanel.tsx:941`) and missed this mount-time one.

          setSessions(list);
          const cur = list[0]; // 按 updated_at 倒序，第一个即最近会话
          setSessionId(cur.id);
+         // 同步镜像（与 switchSession/openExecSession/deleteSession 一致）：
+         // 等不到 effect 拍快照，挂载初瞬时流若到会被旧守卫过滤掉
+         sessionIdRef.current = cur.id;
          const rows = await invoke<
            {
              role: string;
              content: string;
              refsJson?: string | null;
              thinking?: string | null;

─── src/components/ChatPanel/ChatPanel.tsx:960-965 ───
[bug · high] Race condition: the sibling `bot_session_create` branch of the freshly-fixed delete
handler is left unguarded. After `await invoke<Session>("bot_session_create", ...)`,
`setSessionId(s.id)` runs unconditionally — if the user has clicked another session during the await
(e.g. via the session menu, `switchSessionRef`, or ⌘K), the new session will silently steal the
view. The `setSessions([s])` also wipes any sessions the user may have switched to via concurrent
state updates.

Apply the same ref-and-guard pattern that was just added to the `rest.length` branch immediately
above:
```ts
} else {
  // 同步镜像，等待期间守卫必须读到新会话 sid
  let createdSid: string | null = null;
  // ... create
  sessionIdRef.current = s.id;
  createdSid = s.id;
  const s = await invoke<Session>("bot_session_create", { title: null });
  // 等待期用户已切走：丢弃过期创建结果
  if (sessionIdRef.current !== createdSid) return;
  setSessions([s]);
  setSessionId(s.id);
  setMessages([]);
}
```

Cleaner shape: capture the target sid via a `let createdSid = s.id` set AFTER `await` is impossible
(we don't know the sid before the await) — instead set `sessionIdRef.current = null` *before* the
await (or use a placeholder) and compare after. Practically, the only event that can race here is
`switchSession`, which sets `sessionIdRef.current = sid` mid-await — so the proper guard is:
```ts
const sidBefore = sessionIdRef.current;
const s = await invoke<Session>("bot_session_create", { title: null });
if (sessionIdRef.current !== sidBefore) return; // 用户切走了，丢弃
setSessions([s]);
setSessionId(s.id);
sessionIdRef.current = s.id;
setMessages([]);
```
This catches both `switchSessionRef.current(...)` and a future `deleteSession` re-entry.

          } else {
+           // 没有剩余会话 → 新建；用快照 sid 防 await 期被 switchSession 抢走
+           const sidBefore = sessionIdRef.current;
            const s = await invoke<Session>("bot_session_create", { title: null });
+           // 用户在 await 期间切走了：新会话不抢视图（避免旧会话消息瞬间显示又切回）
+           if (sessionIdRef.current !== sidBefore) return;
            setSessions([s]);
            setSessionId(s.id);
+           sessionIdRef.current = s.id;
            setMessages([]);
          }

the assertion entirely and rely on the rest of the test, (b) check for a substring that does occur
naturally (e.g. `d.add_heading(` plus a `level=` parameter) using `str::find` on a regex-like
needle, or (c) use a positive regression guard (e.g. `assert!(src.contains("def add_heading_cjk"))`)
so a missing function would trip it.

[security · high] `doc_make_ppt` silently substitutes the built-in master when the user explicitly
requests a non-existent template. Unlike `doc_make_word` (which logs `tpl_note` for the requested
path), `doc_make_ppt` only logs `doc_make_ppt | out: {out}` and never records which template was
actually applied. A user picking "Corporate.pptx" and getting the built-in midnight-business theme
will see no warning, no audit trail, and the wrong visual style. Resolve the template up-front like
`doc_make_ppt` already does for the success path, log `template = <resolved-or-fallback>` to
`bot.log`, and either surface a soft warning to the caller (preferred) or refuse the request when an
explicit template name does not exist.

-     // 版式随母版：显式模板 → _default 标记 → 内置母版，永不为空
+     let user_template = template.clone();
      let template = match pptx_template_resolve(&app, template.as_deref()) {
          Some(p) => p,
-         None => builtin_pptx_master(&app)?,
+         None => {
+             if user_template.is_some() {
+                 py_audit(
+                     &app,
+                     &format!(
+                         "doc_make_ppt template_fallback | requested={} -> builtin",
+                         crate::audit::escape_for_log(&user_template.unwrap_or_default(), 200)
+                     ),
+                 );
+             }
+             builtin_pptx_master(&app)?
+         }
      };

─── src/components/EvolutionPanel/types.ts:192-198 ───
[maintainability · medium] `DeriveThresholds` 字段是 `mergeMinIds / distillMinIds /
contradictionMinIds`，但 `suggestedThresholds()` 返回的却是短键 `merge / distill /
contradiction`。当前唯一消费者（EvolutionPanel.tsx L425-428）只把建议值渲染成展示文本，所以命名差异不会立刻咬人；但函数被 export
出来、注释里写「按记忆总量给建议值」，将来若有人加「应用建议」按钮 spread 进 form，就会同时丢 `tier` 字段并需要手工 key
映射——是典型的「现在不痛、以后必痛」。建议要么让函数返回 `DeriveThresholds & { tier }`（长键 + tier 旁挂）、要么在 JSDoc 里明确标注「仅供展示，不要
spread 到 DeriveThresholds」。

+ /** 镜像后端 derive.rs DeriveThresholds（camelCase，serde(rename_all = "camelCase")） */
  export interface DeriveThresholds {
    mergeMinIds: number;
    distillMinIds: number;
    contradictionMinIds: number;
  }
  
- export const DERIVE_THRESHOLD_META: {
+ /** 给 EvolutionPanel 直接当 onClick handler 用：键名一致避免手抄映射 */
+ export function suggestedThresholds(total: number): DeriveThresholds & { tier: string } {
+   if (total < TIER_SMALL_MAX) return { mergeMinIds: 2, distillMinIds: 2, contradictionMinIds: 1, tier: "0–100" };
+   if (total < TIER_MID_MAX) return { mergeMinIds: 3, distillMinIds: 3, contradictionMinIds: 1, tier: "100–500" };
+   if (total < TIER_LARGE_MAX) return { mergeMinIds: 3, distillMinIds: 4, contradictionMinIds: 1, tier: "500–2000" };
+   return { mergeMinIds: 4, distillMinIds: 5, contradictionMinIds: 1, tier: "2000+" };
+ }

[bug · medium] 4s auto-clear timer for `thresholdsMsg` is never tracked or cleared. (1) If the panel
unmounts inside 4s, the callback fires `setThresholdsMsg` on an unmounted component (React warning).
(2) Two saves within 4s stack overlapping timers — the earlier one wipes the later success message
early. The codebase has an established fix pattern (see `SkillsPanel.tsx` `noticeTimerRef`,
`McpPanel.tsx` `noticeTimer`, etc.):

```ts
const msgTimerRef = useRef<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null>(null);
useEffect(() => () => { if (msgTimerRef.current) clearTimeout(msgTimerRef.current); }, []);
...
if (msgTimerRef.current) clearTimeout(msgTimerRef.current);
msgTimerRef.current = setTimeout(() => setThresholdsMsg(""), 4000);
```

─── src/components/EvolutionPanel/EvolutionPanel.tsx:104-109 ───
[maintainability · medium] Default thresholds are baked into the frontend (`{ mergeMinIds: 2,
distillMinIds: 2, contradictionMinIds: 1 }`) with a comment claiming "后端同口径". Today the values match
`DeriveThresholds::default()` in `evolution/derive.rs`, but the contract is implicit — if the
backend default ever changes (e.g. merge→3 after observing noise), the panel will silently flash
stale numbers on mount until the invoke resolves, and a user who saves immediately could persist the
stale value. The sibling `applyPolicy` pattern (`useState<ApplyPolicy>("auto")` +
`evolution_get_apply_policy` overwrite) avoids this by deferring to the backend as source of truth.
Either initialize with a `null` sentinel and render a loading state, or load thresholds before first
paint. At minimum, add a test or comment that pins the contract to `DeriveThresholds::default()`.

[style · low] Inconsistent error handling within the same mount effect: `evolution_get_apply_policy`
uses an empty catch with a comment, `evolution_metrics` calls `setMetrics(null)` explicitly, and the
new `evolution_get_thresholds` uses an empty catch with a comment. `mem_stats` does
`setMemTotal(null)`. Three different conventions for the same class of failure in 20 lines. Pick one
(either always silent with a comment, or always assign to state) so the block doesn't drift. Also,
silent catches make future backend regressions invisible — consider logging via `console.warn` at
minimum.

    setBusy(false);
    ...
  }

─── src/components/SettingsPage/MemoryPanel.tsx:662-662 ───
[maintainability · low] `Record<string, number>` does not reflect the actual contract — the backend
`memory_tuning_set` expects `Option<MemoryTuning>` (a typed Rust struct with 8 specific fields). The
flat dict works only because serde tolerates missing fields (via `#[serde(default)]`) and ignores
unknown keys. If a Rust field is renamed/added, this frontend type would silently drift. Define a
typed interface matching the Rust struct fields exactly:

```ts
interface MemoryTuningPayload {
  injectionBudgetChars?: number;
  topN?: number;
  recentN?: number;
  lessonN?: number;
  capacity?: number;
  decayDays?: number;
  dedupMergeCosine?: number;
  dedupHintCosine?: number;
}
```

Then `parseMemoryTuningInput` returns `MemoryTuningPayload | null`, and
`invoke<MemoryTuningPayload>("memory_tuning_get")`.

- const t = await invoke<Record<string, number>>("memory_tuning_get");
+ const t = await invoke<MemoryTuningPayload | null>("memory_tuning_get");

─── src/components/WorkflowCanvas/HoldToConfirmDelete.tsx:229-234 ───
[bug · medium] The 80ms `window.setTimeout(() => void onConfirm(), 80)` is not tracked in any ref
and the cleanup effects only cancel `rafRef` / `shakeTimerRef`. If the component unmounts (or the
parent's `activeId` flips) within that 80ms, `onConfirm()` still runs against a torn-down instance.
This directly contradicts the module docstring ("句柄 ref 跟踪，卸载时清"). None of the existing lifecycle
tests cover the keyboard-80ms-unmount case (only pointer-press and shake-timer are covered), so this
regression would slip through CI. Add a dedicated `confirmTimerRef`, store the handle, and clear it
in the unmount + `disabled` cleanups — mirroring `shakeTimerRef` and `clearShakeTimer`.



─── src/components/WorkflowCanvas/HoldToConfirmDelete.tsx:100-118 ───
[maintainability · medium] There is a logic race in the 360ms window between ring fill and the
`shakeTimerRef` callback. During shake, `phase` is still `"pressing"` because `setPhase("done")` is
deferred to the timer, so the `hold-confirm` button still has normal `pointer-events` (the CSS
`.is-done { pointer-events: none }` rule is gated on `is-done` class). `handlePointerDown` only
blocks re-press when `phaseRef.current === "done"`, so a pointer-down landing during the shake is
accepted and starts a fresh rAF tick. When the original `shakeTimerRef` then fires, it force-sets
`setShaking(false)` + `setPhase("done")` and relies on `confirmFiredRef` to dedupe — visually this
leaves `progress` and `phase` contradictory. Either transition to a dedicated `"shaking"` phase that
the pointer handlers also reject, or have the shake completion cancel/cancel-safe-restart in-flight
RAF in the same path.



─── src/components/WorkflowCanvas/HoldToConfirmDelete.tsx:27-40 ───
[other · low] The rAF-driven progress ring bypasses the global `prefers-reduced-motion` rule
(`src/ui/main.css:112`), which only collapses `transition-duration` / `animation-duration`. The 1.5s
`requestAnimationFrame` loop and the `strokeDashoffset` updates driven from React state still play
out the full duration for users with motion sensitivity. Read
`window.matchMedia("(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)")` once via `useState`, and when reduced motion
is active either fast-forward to 1.0 immediately on pointerdown / keydown or shrink `HOLD_MS` to
e.g. 200ms and use a discrete step. The shake keyframe is already neutralized by the CSS rule, so
this fix only needs to target the JS-driven ring.



─── src/components/WorkflowCanvas/HoldToConfirmDelete.tsx:256-258 ───
[other · low] `aria-busy` is defined as "the element is being modified and that modifications may
not be complete yet" — semantically wrong for the completed `"done"` state where the destructive
action has succeeded. The existing test at line 125 asserts `aria-busy="true"` after completion,
baking in the incorrect mapping. Drop the attribute entirely (the "已删除" label is enough), or —
better for screen-reader users who navigated focus away during the hold — render the label in a
`role="status"` / `aria-live="polite"` live region so the result is announced regardless of focus.

dropped the leading ` * ` continuation prefix, leaving a literal `*` inside the comment body. While
still valid JavaScript (the only `*/` is at the end), the standard JSDoc convention is to start each
continuation line with ` * ` so documentation tooling can parse the comment as multi-line. Restore
the two-line form so each line still begins with ` * ` — single-line form also pushes past typical
100-char line-length caps and hurts readability of the long explanation.

─── src/components/WorkflowCanvas/HoldToConfirmDelete.tsx:97-99 ───
[bug · medium] Re-pressing during the releasing animation causes the ring to jump from its current
progress back to 0 instead of continuing from where it was. The `startProgressRef.current` (passed
via `startTick("pressing", progress)` from `handlePointerDown`) is plumbed through but the
`pressing` branch of `tick` ignores it: `p = elapsed / HOLD_MS` always starts at 0, while the
`releasing` branch correctly uses `startProgressRef.current` as its offset. This is asymmetric with
the symmetric design hinted at by `startTick`'s signature. Scenario: user holds to 0.5, releases
(ring retracts toward 0), then presses again at progress 0.3 → progress snaps to 0 and the ring
disappears, then re-fills over HOLD_MS. The `strokeDashoffset = RING_CIRC * (1 - progress)` makes
this a visible glitch. Fix: use `Math.min(1, startProgressRef.current + elapsed / HOLD_MS)` in the
pressing branch, mirroring the releasing branch's offset-from-start pattern.

        if (cur === "pressing") {
-         const p = Math.min(1, elapsed / HOLD_MS);
+         const p = Math.min(1, startProgressRef.current + elapsed / HOLD_MS);
          setProgress(p);

start of each continuation line; a stray `*` in the body text will break comment folding, hover
docs, and any doc generator that walks the block. The block should be split back onto separate
lines, each starting with ` *`.

[performance · medium] `exec-flow` runs `1.5s linear infinite` unconditionally on every rendered
ExecuteBar. Even when `pct === 0` (the liquid element is clipped to zero width and the diagonal flow
stripes are completely invisible), the compositor keeps repainting the animated background-position
every frame for the entire page lifetime. The test in `ExecuteBar.test.tsx` re-renders the bar in
many states; in production the bar can be mounted across long sessions. Gate the animation to the
running state, or stop the animation entirely when the liquid is empty (e.g. via `.has-liquid` class
— the React component already sets `pct > 0 ? "has-liquid" : ""` but the CSS doesn't use it).

─── src/ui/main.css:802-816 ───
[performance · low] `will-change: width` is applied unconditionally to `.exec-bar__liquid`. Unlike
`transform`/`opacity`, `will-change: width` does not reliably promote a compositor layer; it forces
the browser to keep the element's pre-/post-layout sizes ready. This permanently pins memory and can
cause paint thrash for every mounted bar (the workflow canvas can render many bars over time). Scope
it to active states only, and remove it after the transition completes.

- .exec-bar__liquid {
-   position: absolute;
-   left: 0;
-   top: 0;
-   bottom: 0;
-   width: 0%;
-   /* 不继承圆角：由 .exec-bar 的 overflow:hidden + 圆角裁切，
-      低液位时左端圆/右端直，更像「液体填充」而非孤立的圆球 */
-   background: linear-gradient(180deg, #34d399 0%, #22c55e 44%, #16a34a 100%);
-   box-shadow: inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.45),
-     inset 0 -3px 6px rgba(6, 78, 59, 0.28);

[other · low] `touch-action: none` on `.hold-confirm` and `.save-btn` is the first usage in this
codebase (confirmed via search across all CSS). It correctly prevents the long-press from being
misread as a scroll gesture, but it also disables pinch-zoom and double-tap-zoom within the button
bounds on touch devices. Combined with `user-select: none`, it can make the button feel "dead" to
touch users. The pattern is fine for the long-press case (the comment explains the intent), but the
save button doesn't actually need it — it fires on click, not on hold. Consider dropping it from
`.save-btn` to avoid affecting future touch consumers of the same class.

─── src/ui/main.css:599-606 ───
[other · low] `.hold-confirm-ring-fg` sets `transition: none` to let rAF drive the stroke-dashoffset
animation. That comment is helpful, but if the component ever uses `stroke-dashoffset` as a CSS
variable bound to React state, browsers DO still interpolate the value smoothly even without
`transition`. The `transition: none` is correct here, but make sure the React side doesn't
accidentally set `transition-property: stroke-dashoffset` on the SVG via inheritance from the parent
button's `transition` list — the current button transition doesn't list `stroke-dashoffset`, so this
is safe today. Worth a CSS comment that this is a load-bearing override (not "just in case").

  .hold-confirm-ring-fg {
    fill: none;
    stroke: var(--danger);
    stroke-width: 2.5;
    stroke-linecap: round;
-   /* 关掉 transition：rAF 帧驱动进度，不让 CSS 缓动叠加导致反向时抽搐 */
-   transition: none;
+   /* 关键覆盖：rAF 帧驱动 stroke-dashoffset，禁止 CSS 缓动叠加（反向时会抽搐）。
+      若父级 .hold-confirm 的 transition 列表未来加入 stroke-dashoffset 需同步评估。 */
+   transition: none !important;
  }

[other · medium] `box-shadow: inset 0 2px 4px rgba(23, 32, 51, 0.2)` on `.save-btn.is-pressed`

color, so the pressed indent is barely visible — the visual feedback only really works on light
backgrounds. Consider using a token like `var(--sh-inset)` (or a dedicated inset-shadow token) that
has both `:root` and `.dark` variants, mirroring how `--sh-top`, `--sh-low`, `--sh-high` are already

[bug · high] `.save-btn.is-pressed` is dead-on-arrival during the most common press (mouse-hovered
click). The hover rule `.save-btn:hover:not(:disabled)` has specificity (0,3,0) — `.save-btn`
(0,1,0) + `:hover` (0,1,0) + `:not(:disabled)` (0,1,0) — versus `.save-btn.is-pressed` at (0,2,0).
Because the cursor is still over the button while `is-pressed` is active (pointerdown handler leaves
the pointer captured at the same coordinates), the higher-specificity hover rule wins on
`background-color` and `color`, leaving only the `transform` (translateY 1px) and `box-shadow`
(inset shadow) as the visible "pressed" cue. The intended `background-color: var(--inset-bg)` and
`color: var(--t1)` never paint, so users get a hover-tinted bottom + a 1px translate instead of an
obvious pressed indent. Either bump `.is-pressed` specificity (`hold-confirm.is-pressed` →
`.save-btn.save-btn.is-pressed`, or compound with `:not(:hover)`, or set values in a
`:not(:hover)`-clamped variant) or move these properties into a state that excludes hover
(`.save-btn.is-pressed:not(:hover)`).

- .save-btn.is-pressed {
+ /* 按下 + 未 hover：避免 hover 规则覆盖 pressed 底色 */
+ .save-btn.is-pressed:not(:hover) {
    background-color: var(--inset-bg);
-   box-shadow: inset 0 2px 4px rgba(23, 32, 51, 0.2);
+   box-shadow: inset 0 2px 4px color-mix(in srgb, var(--t1) 20%, transparent);
    color: var(--t1);
    transform: translateY(1px);
  }

var(--edge-strong)`. The "red border while holding" affordance never paints. Boost `.is-active`
